## Aether OS— Virtual Cloud Aggregator: Architecture RFC

_Revision 0.1 · Classification: Internal Engineering · Audience: Staff+_

### Part 1 · Executive Technical Summary

**The single hardest problem:** Maintaining a causally-consistent virtual filesystem view across 4 providers with different eventual-consistency models, no atomic cross-provider transactions, and files that can be mutated out-of-band directly in each provider's native UI.

**One-sentence solution:** Model the inode tree as a CRDT-based append-only event log where each provider's change-token stream (Google Drive Changes API, OneDrive delta link, Dropbox longpoll) is ingested as a causal event source, and conflicts are resolved by a deterministic, client-side merge function — meaning no server ever arbitrates on plaintext structure.

### Part 2 · Technology Stack

| Component | Selected Technology | Justification |
|---|---|---|
| Primary Backend | Rust 1.78 (tokio + tonic) | Memory safety is non-negotiable on crypto hot paths; zero-cost async handles the 4× provider fan-out per write without thread explosion; single static binary simplifies K8s deployment |
| Secondary/Utility | TypeScript / Node 20 LTS | All 4 provider SDKs are JS-native; OAuth PKCE flows, webhook ingestion, delta-sync polling naturally fit TS's I/O model |
| Frontend Web | Next.js 14 (App Router) | RSC for near-zero TTFB on metadata-heavy file-tree views; Edge Runtime for session validation without round-trip to auth service |
| Mobile | Expo + React Native (New Arch) | Shares crypto-core package via JSI-based WASM bridge; avoids duplicating the entire encryption pipeline in Swift/Kotlin |
| Desktop | Tauri 2.0 | Directly embeds Rust backend crates; FUSE (Linux/macOS) + Dokan (Windows) for native OS mount point; 8 MB bundle vs Electron's 150 MB |
| Metadata DB | CockroachDB 23.x | Serializable isolation for inode writes without multi-region 2PC pain; geo-partitioning satisfies GDPR data residency; proven at scale that CockroachDB's own storage layer handles |
| Cache | Redis 7 Cluster | Sorted sets for rate-limit token bucket state per (user, provider) pair; keyspace notifications for invalidating hot-inode cache on delta-sync events; sub-ms read path for directory listings |
| Message Queue | NATS JetStream 2.10 | Native priority consumer groups implement the P0–P3 queue natively; 3× lower per-message overhead than Kafka at our 10M op/s target; built-in KV store absorbs token bucket persistence without a separate Redis key namespace |
| Internal Object Storage | Cloudflare R2 (staging only) | Zero egress fees for server-side staging of in-flight multi-chunk uploads before fan-out; providers ARE the persistent storage layer |
| Container Orchestration | AWS EKS + Karpenter | Karpenter's just-in-time node provisioning is mandatory for handling upload traffic bursts without 10-minute ASG warmup |
| Service Mesh / API GW | Istio + Envoy (internal mTLS), Kong (external) | mTLS prevents cross-service data extraction in a zero-trust posture; Kong handles external rate-limiting, API key rotation, and JWT validation at the edge |
| Observability | OpenTelemetry → Grafana Stack (Tempo + Loki + Mimir) | Vendor-neutral; Tempo's trace-to-log correlation is essential for debugging partial shard upload failures across 4 providers in a single request span |

### Part 3 · Full System Architecture

The core architectural invariant: **the control plane is authoritative over metadata and scheduling; the data plane never transmits plaintext to any server.**

The visualizer timed out. I'll represent the architecture as a structured code block and continue with all remaining parts.

```
╔══════════════════════════ CONTROL PLANE ════════════════════════════╗
│  ┌─────────────────┐  ┌───────────────────────┐  ┌───────────────┐ │
│  │  Auth service   │  │    Metadata / KMS     │  │ Rate-limit Q  │ │
│  │  OAuth 2.0·JWT  │  │ inode·ZK keys·Δ-sync  │  │  P0–P3 lanes  │ │
│  └────────┬────────┘  └──────────┬────────────┘  └───────────────┘ │
╚═══════════╪═══════════════════════╪══ ZK boundary (plaintext stops) ╝
            ↓ (session tokens)      ↓ (wrapped DEKs + inode refs)
╔══════════════════════════ DATA PLANE (ZK) ══════════════════════════╗
│  ┌──────────────────┐ → ┌──────────────────┐ → ┌────────────────┐ │
│  │  Client (WASM)   │   │ Chunk orchestrator│   │ Providers      │ │
│  │  Argon2id        │   │ erasure fan-out   │   │ · Google Drive │ │
│  │  AES-256-GCM     │   │ 10+4 RS shards    │   │ · OneDrive     │ │
│  │  RS 10+4 · X25519│   │ quota-gated       │   │ · Dropbox      │ │
│  └──────────────────┘   └──────────────────┘   │ · MEGA         │ │
│                                                  └────────────────┘ │
╚═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝
       All provider calls: mTLS. Providers see encrypted opaque blobs.
```

**Write path — 5 GB file:**

1. Client generates a random 256-bit DEK, wraps it with KEK (`HKDF(MSK, file_id)`), and stores `encrypted_DEK` in the metadata service.

2. `crypto-core` WASM splits the file into 64 MB logical chunks (~78 chunks for 5 GB), then applies RS(10,4) to each chunk → 14 shards of ~6.4 MB each.

3. Each shard is encrypted: `AES-256-GCM(DEK, shard_bytes, AAD=chunk_index‖shard_index)`.

4. Client calls chunk orchestrator with the 14-shard manifest. Orchestrator consults the rate-limit queue (`P1 = user save`) and acquires tokens for each `(user, provider)` bucket.

5. 14 shards fan out across ≥2 providers. With 4 providers, the scheduler distributes as roughly 4–4–3–3 shards/provider, ensuring any 2 providers going offline still leaves 6+ shards (RS needs 10).

6. On success, orchestrator writes `physical_chunks` rows to CockroachDB. Metadata service updates the inode's `current_version_id`.

**Read/reconstruction path during throttling:**

1. Client requests inode. Metadata service returns `physical_chunks` manifest.

2. Chunk orchestrator issues shard-fetch requests at `P0` priority. If provider A returns HTTP 429, the orchestrator immediately re-routes to alternative shards (RS only needs any 10 of 14) without blocking.

3. With 4 providers at 10 req/s each = 40 req/s burst budget for a single user. At 6.4 MB/shard × 10 parallel shards = 64 MB/s sustained read throughput before hitting any provider limit — well above practical consumer bandwidth.

4. Shard reassembly and AES-GCM decryption happen client-side in the WASM module. Server never touches plaintext.

### Part 4 · Core Storage Engine Design

**Chunking & Erasure Coding — RS(10, 4):**

10 data shards + 4 parity shards per logical chunk. Rationale: tolerates loss of any 4 shards simultaneously — meaning 2 complete provider outages (2 providers × 3–4 shards each) with zero data loss and no degraded reads. 40% storage overhead is acceptable given providers offer free tier storage. RS(6,2) was rejected: survives only 1 full provider outage. RS(4,2) is 50% overhead with less resilience. The 64 MB chunk size is the sweet spot — large enough to amortize API call overhead (~$0.0001/API call at volume), small enough to fit in client WASM heap without triggering GC pressure on mobile.

WASM library: `reed-solomon-erasure` (Rust crate, compiled to WASM target `wasm32-unknown-unknown`), wrapped by `packages/crypto-core/src/erasure/ReedSolomon.ts`.

**Encryption Pipeline — KEK/DEK hierarchy:**

```
password ──Argon2id(t=3, m=65536, p=4, salt=user_salt)──→ MSK (256-bit, never leaves device)
MSK ──HKDF-SHA256(info="kek_v1")──────────────────────────→ KEK (256-bit, never leaves device)
KEK ──AES-256-GCM(random DEK, nonce=random)───────────────→ encrypted_DEK (stored server-side)
DEK ──AES-256-GCM(shard_bytes, AAD=chunk_idx‖shard_idx)───→ ciphertext shard (sent to provider)
```

The AAD binding `chunk_index‖shard_index` prevents shard substitution attacks — a compromised provider cannot swap shard positions without breaking the AEAD tag. Server stores only `encrypted_DEK` and `checksum=BLAKE3(ciphertext_shard)`.

**Metadata Consistency — out-of-band change detection:**

Each provider exposes a change-token stream:

- Google Drive: Changes API with `pageToken`

- OneDrive: `/delta` with `deltaToken`

- Dropbox: `/files/list_folder/longpoll`

- MEGA: WebSocket event push via their SDK

The `delta-sync-service` (part of Metadata service) maintains per-`(user, provider)` cursor tokens in Redis. On each poll (interval: 30s background, immediate on webhook push), it receives change events, matches `provider_object_id` against `physical_chunks`, marks affected `inode_version_id` rows as `dirty`, and enqueues a `P3` reconciliation task. The reconciliation re-fetches the shard, verifies `BLAKE3(fetched_bytes) == stored_checksum`, and either accepts the out-of-band write (if user authorized) or alerts and triggers re-upload.

### Part 5 · Zero-Knowledge Security Model

**KMS design:** Stratum operates a "bring-your-own-key" model entirely on the client. MSK is derived on device, KEK never leaves memory, and DEKs are wrapped before any network call. The server's "KMS" is a misnomer — it stores wrapped DEKs (opaque blobs), rotation metadata, and public keys for sharing. It cannot unwrap any DEK.

Key rotation: re-derive new DEK per file version on every save. The old `encrypted_DEK` is retained in `inode_versions` for audited rollback.

**Sharing files without re-encrypting blobs:**

Hybrid encryption over the DEK, not the blob:

1. Recipient publishes an X25519 public key (registered during account setup).

2. Sharer: generates ephemeral X25519 keypair `(eph_priv, eph_pub)`, computes `shared_secret = X25519(eph_priv, recipient_pub)`, derives `share_key = HKDF(shared_secret, "share_v1")`.

3. Encrypts DEK: `ChaCha20-Poly1305(share_key, DEK)` → `share_token`.

4. Stores `(share_token, eph_pub)` in the metadata service. Recipient decrypts `share_key = HKDF(X25519(recipient_priv, eph_pub))`, unwraps DEK.

5. The blob never moves, never gets re-encrypted. Revocation = delete the `share_token` row.

**Audit log strategy:**

Append-only operation log signed with the user's Ed25519 key (derived from MSK via `HKDF("audit_signing_key")`). Each entry: `{timestamp, op_type, inode_id, blake3_content_hash, signature}`. Hash-chained: each entry includes `prev_entry_hash`. Server stores the opaque chain for abuse detection (e.g. rate anomaly on `download` ops suggesting exfiltration) without reading plaintext. For CSAM/compliance scanning: zero-knowledge and abuse prevention are in direct tension — mitigation is PhotoDNA-style perceptual hash commitment schemes (out of scope for v1).

### Part 6 · Provider Rate-Limit Queue Design

```
NATS JetStream subjects:
  provider.{provider_id}.{user_id}.p0  ← interactive reads       (drain first)
  provider.{provider_id}.{user_id}.p1  ← user-initiated writes
  provider.{provider_id}.{user_id}.p2  ← background replication
  provider.{provider_id}.{user_id}.p3  ← delta-sync / integrity checks
```

Each `(user, provider)` pair has a **token bucket** in Redis:

- Capacity: 10 tokens (Google's 10 req/s user limit)

- Refill: 1 token/100ms

- Implementation: Redis `ZADD` + Lua atomic CAS script (prevents race conditions under distributed workers)

**Consumer group per priority lane.** The chunk-orchestrator process subscribes to P0 first, drains it to empty, then P1, etc. If a provider returns `HTTP 429`, the job is NACKed back to the queue with exponential backoff (base 2s, max 60s) and the token bucket is force-drained to 0 (assume server-side quota reset window). P2/P3 jobs are automatically deprioritized behind any live P0/P1 work.

**Circuit breaker:** After 5 consecutive 429s in a 60-second window, the `(user, provider)` combination is soft-blocked for 5 minutes. Outstanding P2/P3 work for that provider is silently deferred. P0/P1 work is rerouted to alternate shard sources (RS guarantees availability from remaining providers).

### Part 7 · Database Schema

```sql
CREATE TABLE volumes (
    id              UUID        PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    user_id         UUID        NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    name            TEXT        NOT NULL,
    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    total_logical_bytes BIGINT  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    encryption_config   JSONB   NOT NULL,   -- {kek_version, algorithm, argon2_params}
    INDEX idx_volumes_user (user_id)
);

CREATE TABLE inodes (
    id              UUID        PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    volume_id       UUID        NOT NULL REFERENCES volumes(id),
    parent_id       UUID        REFERENCES inodes(id),          -- NULL = root
    name            TEXT        NOT NULL,
    inode_type      TEXT        NOT NULL CHECK (inode_type IN ('file','directory','symlink')),
    size_bytes      BIGINT,
    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    modified_at     TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    content_hash    BYTEA,      -- encrypted BLAKE3; server cannot verify plaintext integrity
    current_version_id UUID,    -- FK to inode_versions (set after first write)
    UNIQUE (parent_id, name),
    INDEX idx_inodes_volume   (volume_id),
    INDEX idx_inodes_parent   (parent_id),
    INDEX idx_inodes_modified (modified_at DESC)   -- delta-sync range scans
);

-- Represents one committed file version (immutable after write)
CREATE TABLE inode_versions (
    id              UUID        PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    inode_id        UUID        NOT NULL REFERENCES inodes(id),
    version_number  INT         NOT NULL,
    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    total_chunks    INT         NOT NULL,
    UNIQUE (inode_id, version_number)
);

CREATE TABLE physical_chunks (
    id                  UUID    PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    inode_version_id    UUID    NOT NULL REFERENCES inode_versions(id),
    chunk_index         INT     NOT NULL,       -- logical chunk position
    shard_index         INT     NOT NULL,       -- 0..13 for RS(10,4)
    provider_id         TEXT    NOT NULL,       -- 'google_drive' | 'onedrive' | 'dropbox' | 'mega'
    provider_object_id  TEXT    NOT NULL,       -- opaque ID in provider's namespace
    provider_etag       TEXT,                   -- for out-of-band change detection
    size_bytes          BIGINT  NOT NULL,
    encrypted_dek       BYTEA   NOT NULL,       -- AES-256-GCM(KEK, DEK)
    checksum            BYTEA   NOT NULL,       -- BLAKE3 of encrypted shard bytes
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    UNIQUE (inode_version_id, chunk_index, shard_index),
    INDEX idx_chunks_version    (inode_version_id),
    INDEX idx_chunks_provider   (provider_id, provider_object_id)  -- delta-sync lookup
);

-- Note: oauth tokens encrypted with a server-side AES key (not ZK — unavoidable;
-- providers need live refresh tokens to operate on the user's behalf).
CREATE TABLE provider_credentials (
    id                  UUID    PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    user_id             UUID    NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
    provider_id         TEXT    NOT NULL,
    encrypted_tokens    BYTEA   NOT NULL,       -- AES-256-GCM(server_kek, token_json)
    token_iv            BYTEA   NOT NULL,
    scopes              TEXT[]  NOT NULL,
    connected_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    last_refreshed_at   TIMESTAMPTZ,
    storage_quota_bytes BIGINT,
    storage_used_bytes  BIGINT,
    UNIQUE (user_id, provider_id)
);
```

### Part 9 · Cost Estimate & Scaling Math

**First 1,000 users — monthly burn:**

| Line item | Cost |
|---|---|
| EKS: 3× t3.large (chunk-orchestrator, metadata svc, rate-limit svc) | $145 |
| CockroachDB Serverless (up to 50M RUs/month free, then $0.20/1M) | $80 |
| ElastiCache cache.t4g.small × 2 (Redis cluster) | $55 |
| Cloudflare R2 (staging blobs, ~500 GB/month at zero egress) | $8 |
| NATS JetStream (self-hosted on EKS, no incremental cost) | $0 |
| CloudFront CDN + Route53 | $25 |
| Misc (ACM, ECR, CloudWatch logs) | $30 |
| Total | ~$343/month |

Key insight: provider API calls are free; the user's own Google/OneDrive/Dropbox storage absorbs all blob costs.

**Scaling breaker at 1M MAU:**

The metadata database IOPS fails first, not egress. At 1M MAU doing ~50 file operations/day average = 50M ops/day = ~580 IOPS sustained, peaking at 10–20× = 6,000–12,000 IOPS during business hours. Each file operation touches 3–4 tables (inode, inode_versions, physical_chunks × 14 rows). That's 42,000–56,000 actual row-level IOPS at peak. CockroachDB Serverless's RU budget at $500/month will not cover this — you need CockroachDB Dedicated at ~$8,000–12,000/month for that IOPS tier.

Egress is survivable because blobs flow provider-to-client directly, not through your infrastructure. Your egress cost is metadata JSON only, which is negligible.

### Part 10 · MVP Roadmap (30/60/90 Days)

**Day 30 deliverable — CLI encryption pipeline proof:**

`tools/cli` (`tsx`-runnable): connects to one provider (Google Drive), authenticates via OAuth PKCE local server, takes a local file, runs the full `crypto-core` pipeline (Argon2id key derivation → DEK generation → AES-256-GCM encrypt → RS(10,4) shard → upload 14 shards), then round-trips the download+decrypt+verify path. Exit code 0 = crypto pipeline is correct, provider integration works, rate-limit queue fires. No web UI, no monorepo build tooling needed. This is the riskiest assumption to validate first.

**Day 60:** Add CockroachDB inode schema, second provider (OneDrive), and `delta-sync-service` for one provider. Prove out-of-band change detection works.

**Day 90:** Next.js web app, OAuth for all 4 providers, virtual file tree UI with upload/download. Internal beta.

**Feature you MUST delete to ship Year 1:** Real-time collaborative editing and conflict resolution on shared files. Operational transforms + CRDT merge over encrypted content is an unsolved UX problem (you can't show a diff of ciphertext). This feature will consume 6+ months and block the entire encryption team. Defer to Year 2 with a clear "files are locked during editing" MVP model.

### Part 11 · Risk & Honest Odds

**ToS risk — specific clause:**

Google Drive ToS Section 6 ("Your Content in our Services") combined with their API Terms of Service Section 6.3 prohibits "using the API to build a product or service that replicates or substitutes for Google Drive's core functionality." More dangerous: Google Drive API's "per-user rate limits" are enforced at the API Console project level — if Stratum's OAuth app is flagged for bulk automated access patterns resembling redistribution, Google can revoke the API project entirely, taking down all users simultaneously. **This is an existential single point of failure.** Mitigation: keep operations strictly user-delegated (each user's OAuth token; no service accounts), never cache blobs on Stratum servers, consult Google's partnership team early, and architect for provider fallback (design so the product degrades gracefully if one provider suspends access).

MEGA has an additional complexity: their E2EE is implemented in their own SDK (`megajs`), and their API is not officially documented. Integration is brittle.

**Success probability to $10M ARR:** **7%**

Contributing factors to the low number: (1) Google or Microsoft can unilaterally kill the product via ToS enforcement with 30 days' notice — no legal recourse. (2) The target user already has Dropbox/Drive/OneDrive and doesn't perceive fragmentation as a pain point worth $9/month. (3) Zero-knowledge makes customer support nearly impossible — you cannot debug "my file won't open" without decrypting the user's data. (4) MEGA already does encrypted cloud storage natively and charges less. The 7% scenario requires finding a B2B angle (e.g., MSPs managing multiple client cloud accounts) where the aggregation value prop is more concrete.

### Part 12 · Monetization & GTM

**Logical Capacity wins over Connected Accounts pricing.** Connected Accounts pricing (e.g., "$3/account/month") actively incentivizes users to create fake Google accounts to circumvent the free tier — directly accelerating ToS violations that could kill the product. It also doesn't align cost with actual infrastructure burden: a user with 4 empty accounts costs Stratum nothing in metadata IOPS; a user with 4 accounts and 4 TB of files costs significantly more in chunk manifest reads.

**Recommended tiers:**

| Tier | Price | Limits |
|---|---|---|
| Free | $0 | 10 GB logical, 2 providers, 1 device |
| Pro | $9/month | 2 TB logical, unlimited providers, 5 devices |
| Teams | $22/user/month | 10 TB pooled, admin console, audit log export, priority support |

**GTM wedge:** Target privacy-conscious prosumers and SMB IT admins first (not consumers). The zero-knowledge story resonates in regulated industries (legal, healthcare adjacent). Cold outreach to r/selfhosted and privacy-tech communities for organic early adopters; then Product Hunt launch at Day 90 demo. The open-source `crypto-core` library released independently of the product builds developer trust and drives inbound.

_Document status: RFC — pending review from security, infra, and product leads._
_Estimated engineering headcount to reach Day 90: 4 engineers (2 fullstack, 1 infra/SRE, 1 security/crypto specialist)._

___

### Step 2 · Local Control Plane (`docker-compose.yml`)

#### `/infra/docker-compose.yml`

```yaml
name: aether-local

services:

  # ── CockroachDB (single-node, insecure mode for local dev) ──────────────────
  cockroachdb:
    image: cockroachdb/cockroach:v23.2.5
    container_name: aether-crdb
    command: start-single-node --insecure --store=type=mem,size=4GiB
    ports:
      - "26257:26257"   # SQL wire (psql / sqlx)
      - "8080:8080"     # Admin UI → http://localhost:8080
    environment:
      COCKROACH_DATABASE: aether
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "cockroach", "node", "status", "--insecure", "--host=localhost:26257"]
      interval: 10s
      timeout: 5s
      retries: 10
      start_period: 20s
    volumes:
      - crdb-data:/cockroach/cockroach-data
      - ./init/crdb:/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d   # runs *.sql on first boot

  # ── DragonflyDB — Redis-compatible, higher throughput for token buckets ──────
  dragonfly:
    image: docker.dragonflydb.io/dragonflydb/dragonfly:v1.18.0
    container_name: aether-redis
    command: >
      --maxmemory 1gb
      --save ""
      --requirepass ${REDIS_PASSWORD:-aether_local_secret}
      --cluster_mode emulated
    ulimits:
      memlock: -1
    ports:
      - "6379:6379"
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "-a", "${REDIS_PASSWORD:-aether_local_secret}", "PING"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 10
    volumes:
      - dragonfly-data:/data

  # ── NATS JetStream ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  nats:
    image: nats:2.10.16-alpine
    container_name: aether-nats
    command: >
      -js
      -sd /data
      -m 8222
      -c /etc/nats/nats.conf
    ports:
      - "4222:4222"   # client
      - "8222:8222"   # HTTP monitoring → http://localhost:8222
      - "6222:6222"   # cluster routing (unused in single-node dev)
    healthcheck:
      test: ["CMD", "wget", "--quiet", "--tries=1", "--spider", "http://localhost:8222/healthz"]
      interval: 5s
      timeout: 3s
      retries: 10
    volumes:
      - nats-data:/data
      - ./init/nats/nats.conf:/etc/nats/nats.conf:ro

  # ── Initialization sidecar — runs once to seed CRDB schema + NATS streams ──
  aether-init:
    image: cockroachdb/cockroach:v23.2.5
    container_name: aether-init
    depends_on:
      cockroachdb: { condition: service_healthy }
      nats:        { condition: service_healthy }
    environment:
      CRDB_HOST: cockroachdb:26257
    volumes:
      - ./init/crdb:/migrations:ro
      - ./init/seed.sh:/seed.sh:ro
    entrypoint: ["/bin/sh", "/seed.sh"]
    restart: "no"

volumes:
  crdb-data:
  dragonfly-data:
  nats-data:
```

#### `/infra/init/nats/nats.conf`

```
# JetStream configuration for local dev
jetstream {
  store_dir: /data
  max_memory: 512MB
  max_file:   4GB
}

# Pre-declare streams so services don't need to create them at startup
# Actual stream creation is done in seed.sh via nats CLI for predictability
```

#### `/infra/init/seed.sh`

```bash
#!/bin/sh
set -e

echo "==> Waiting for CockroachDB..."
until cockroach sql --insecure --host="${CRDB_HOST}" -e "SELECT 1;" > /dev/null 2>&1; do
  sleep 2
done

echo "==> Running migrations..."
for f in /migrations/*.sql; do
  echo "    Applying: $f"
  cockroach sql --insecure --host="${CRDB_HOST}" --database=aether < "$f" || true
done

echo "==> Creating NATS JetStream streams..."
# Use nats CLI if available; otherwise skip — services self-provision
if command -v nats > /dev/null 2>&1; then
  nats --server=nats://aether-nats:4222 stream add PROVIDER_QUEUE \
    --subjects "provider.>" \
    --storage  file \
    --replicas 1 \
    --retention limits \
    --max-age   24h \
    --discard   old \
    --defaults 2>/dev/null || echo "    Stream PROVIDER_QUEUE already exists, skipping."
fi

echo "==> Aether local environment ready."
```

#### `/infra/init/crdb/V0__create_database.sql`

```sql
-- Run before the main schema; creates the database if it doesn't exist.
CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS aether;
USE aether;
```

### Step 3 · Database Schema Migration

#### Install `sqlx-cli`

```bash
# sqlx-cli with postgres feature covers CockroachDB's psql wire protocol
cargo install sqlx-cli --no-default-features --features postgres,rustls

# Point at local CRDB
export DATABASE_URL="postgresql://root@localhost:26257/aether?sslmode=disable"

# Initialize migrations directory (creates _sqlx_migrations tracking table)
sqlx migrate add --source services/metadata-service/migrations initial_schema
# This creates: services/metadata-service/migrations/V1__{timestamp}__initial_schema.sql
```

#### `services/metadata-service/migrations/V1__initial_schema.sql`

```sql
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Aether OS · V1 Initial Schema
-- Target:  CockroachDB 23.x (PostgreSQL wire-compatible)
-- Notes:   - gen_random_uuid() is native to CockroachDB.
--          - TIMESTAMPTZ stored in UTC; application layer converts to user tz.
--          - physical_chunks intentionally has NO FK to provider_credentials
--            to allow credential rotation without cascading deletes on chunks.
-- ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

-- ── users ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Minimal stub; auth-service owns the full user record.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS users (
    id           UUID        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    email        TEXT        NOT NULL,
    created_at   TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    CONSTRAINT pk_users PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT uq_users_email UNIQUE (email)
);

-- ── volumes ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS volumes (
    id                   UUID        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    user_id              UUID        NOT NULL,
    name                 TEXT        NOT NULL,
    created_at           TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at           TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    total_logical_bytes  INT8        NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    -- Stores JSON: { "kek_version": 1, "algorithm": "AES-256-GCM",
    --                "argon2_params": { "t": 3, "m": 65536, "p": 4 } }
    encryption_config    JSONB       NOT NULL,
    is_deleted           BOOL        NOT NULL DEFAULT false,

    CONSTRAINT pk_volumes PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_volumes_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id)
        REFERENCES users (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    CONSTRAINT uq_volumes_user_name UNIQUE (user_id, name)
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_volumes_user_id
    ON volumes (user_id)
    WHERE is_deleted = false;

-- ── inodes ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
CREATE TYPE IF NOT EXISTS inode_type AS ENUM ('file', 'directory', 'symlink');

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS inodes (
    id                  UUID        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    volume_id           UUID        NOT NULL,
    -- NULL parent_id = volume root directory
    parent_id           UUID,
    name                TEXT        NOT NULL,
    inode_type          inode_type  NOT NULL,
    size_bytes          INT8        NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    modified_at         TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    -- BLAKE3 of encrypted content; server cannot verify plaintext integrity.
    -- NULL for directories.
    content_hash        BYTEA,
    -- Points to the latest committed version; NULL until first write completes.
    current_version_id  UUID,
    -- Soft delete; preserves audit trail and undelete capability.
    is_deleted          BOOL        NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
    -- Tracks CRDT vector clock for out-of-band change merge.
    -- Format: { "local": <lamport_ts>, "providers": { "gdrive": <token>, ... } }
    sync_state          JSONB       NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}',

    CONSTRAINT pk_inodes PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_inodes_volume FOREIGN KEY (volume_id)
        REFERENCES volumes (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    CONSTRAINT fk_inodes_parent FOREIGN KEY (parent_id)
        REFERENCES inodes (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    -- Enforce unique filenames per directory; NULL parent handled by coalesce.
    CONSTRAINT uq_inodes_parent_name UNIQUE (volume_id, parent_id, name)
);

-- Hierarchical traversal: fetch all children of a directory.
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_inodes_parent_id
    ON inodes (volume_id, parent_id)
    WHERE is_deleted = false;

-- Delta-sync range scan: find inodes modified since last poll timestamp.
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_inodes_modified_at
    ON inodes (volume_id, modified_at DESC)
    WHERE is_deleted = false;

-- ── inode_versions ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Immutable once written. Enables rollback without re-downloading blobs.
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS inode_versions (
    id              UUID        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    inode_id        UUID        NOT NULL,
    version_number  INT4        NOT NULL,
    created_at      TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    total_chunks    INT4        NOT NULL,
    -- Wrapped DEK for this version: AES-256-GCM(KEK, DEK).
    -- Server stores the ciphertext; cannot unwrap without KEK (client-only).
    encrypted_dek   BYTEA       NOT NULL,
    -- Nonce used when wrapping DEK; must be unique per wrapping operation.
    dek_nonce       BYTEA       NOT NULL,

    CONSTRAINT pk_inode_versions PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_inode_versions_inode FOREIGN KEY (inode_id)
        REFERENCES inodes (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    CONSTRAINT uq_inode_versions_number UNIQUE (inode_id, version_number)
);

-- Add deferred FK from inodes back to inode_versions (circular; safe at commit).
ALTER TABLE inodes
    ADD CONSTRAINT IF NOT EXISTS fk_inodes_current_version
    FOREIGN KEY (current_version_id)
    REFERENCES inode_versions (id)
    ON DELETE SET NULL;

-- ── physical_chunks ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- One row per erasure-coded shard (RS 10+4 = 14 rows per logical chunk).
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS physical_chunks (
    id                  UUID        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    inode_version_id    UUID        NOT NULL,
    chunk_index         INT4        NOT NULL,  -- logical chunk position (0-based)
    shard_index         INT4        NOT NULL,  -- RS shard position: 0..13 for RS(10,4)
    is_parity           BOOL        NOT NULL
        GENERATED ALWAYS AS (shard_index >= 10) STORED,
    provider_id         TEXT        NOT NULL,  -- 'google_drive'|'onedrive'|'dropbox'|'mega'
    provider_object_id  TEXT        NOT NULL,  -- opaque object ID in provider's namespace
    provider_etag       TEXT,                  -- used for out-of-band change detection
    size_bytes          INT8        NOT NULL,
    -- BLAKE3 hash of the *encrypted* shard bytes; verified on read.
    checksum            BYTEA       NOT NULL,
    created_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    -- Soft state for repair scheduling: 'healthy'|'missing'|'corrupt'|'repairing'
    shard_status        TEXT        NOT NULL DEFAULT 'healthy',

    CONSTRAINT pk_physical_chunks PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_chunks_version FOREIGN KEY (inode_version_id)
        REFERENCES inode_versions (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    -- Exactly one shard per (version, chunk, shard_index).
    CONSTRAINT uq_chunks_shard UNIQUE (inode_version_id, chunk_index, shard_index),
    CONSTRAINT chk_provider_id CHECK (
        provider_id IN ('google_drive', 'onedrive', 'dropbox', 'mega')
    ),
    CONSTRAINT chk_shard_status CHECK (
        shard_status IN ('healthy', 'missing', 'corrupt', 'repairing')
    )
);

-- Primary read path: given a version_id, fetch all 14 shards per chunk.
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_chunks_version_chunk
    ON physical_chunks (inode_version_id, chunk_index, shard_index);

-- Delta-sync lookup: map a provider's native object ID back to our inode.
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_chunks_provider_object
    ON physical_chunks (provider_id, provider_object_id);

-- Repair scheduler: find all unhealthy shards.
CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_chunks_status
    ON physical_chunks (shard_status)
    WHERE shard_status != 'healthy';

-- ── provider_credentials ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- NOTE: OAuth tokens are NOT zero-knowledge. They must be decryptable
-- server-side so background services (delta-sync, repair) can act on the
-- user's behalf. Encrypted with a server-side KEK (HSM-backed in prod).
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS provider_credentials (
    id                  UUID        NOT NULL DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
    user_id             UUID        NOT NULL,
    provider_id         TEXT        NOT NULL,
    -- AES-256-GCM(server_kek, JSON{ access_token, refresh_token, expires_at })
    encrypted_tokens    BYTEA       NOT NULL,
    -- Random 96-bit nonce; unique per encryption operation.
    token_nonce         BYTEA       NOT NULL,
    -- OAuth scopes granted by the user; validated on use.
    scopes              TEXT[]      NOT NULL,
    connected_at        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    last_refreshed_at   TIMESTAMPTZ,
    -- Cached from provider's storage quota API; refreshed hourly.
    storage_quota_bytes INT8,
    storage_used_bytes  INT8,
    -- Soft-revoke: allows clean audit trail without hard delete.
    is_active           BOOL        NOT NULL DEFAULT true,

    CONSTRAINT pk_provider_credentials PRIMARY KEY (id),
    CONSTRAINT fk_creds_user FOREIGN KEY (user_id)
        REFERENCES users (id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
    CONSTRAINT uq_creds_user_provider UNIQUE (user_id, provider_id),
    CONSTRAINT chk_creds_provider_id CHECK (
        provider_id IN ('google_drive', 'onedrive', 'dropbox', 'mega')
    )
);

CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_creds_user_id
    ON provider_credentials (user_id)
    WHERE is_active = true;
```

#### Run the migration

```bash
export DATABASE_URL="postgresql://root@localhost:26257/aether?sslmode=disable"

# Ensure the database exists first (our V0 seed handles this, but double-check)
sqlx database create

# Apply all pending migrations
sqlx migrate run --source services/metadata-service/migrations

# Verify
sqlx migrate info --source services/metadata-service/migrations
# Every migration should show "Applied"
```

### Step 4 · Rust `crypto-core` Scaffold

#### Install WASM toolchain

```bash
rustup target add wasm32-unknown-unknown
cargo install wasm-pack     # WASM build + npm package bundler
cargo install cargo-watch   # hot-reload during development
```

#### Initialize the crate

```bash
cd packages/crypto-core

# Initialize as a Rust library (not a binary)
cargo init --lib --name aether-crypto-core .
```

#### `packages/crypto-core/Cargo.toml`

```toml
[package]
name    = "aether-crypto-core"
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2021"
description = "Zero-knowledge cryptography pipeline for Project Aether"
license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0"

# Compile as both a Rust library (for native services) and a WASM module.
[lib]
name      = "aether_crypto_core"
crate-type = ["cdylib", "rlib"]
#   cdylib → .wasm output (for wasm-bindgen / npm)
#   rlib   → Rust library (for linking into metadata-service, chunk-orchestrator)

# ── Core cryptography ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
[dependencies]
# AES-256-GCM authenticated encryption
aes-gcm = { version = "0.10", features = ["aes", "alloc"] }

# Argon2id for password-based key derivation (MSK)
argon2 = { version = "0.5", features = ["alloc", "password-hash"] }

# HKDF-SHA256 for deterministic key derivation (KEK, sub-keys)
hkdf = "0.12"
sha2 = "0.10"

# ChaCha20-Poly1305 for DEK sharing tokens (faster on platforms without AES-NI)
chacha20poly1305 = { version = "0.10", features = ["alloc"] }

# X25519 ECDH (ephemeral key exchange for file sharing)
x25519-dalek = { version = "2", features = ["static_secrets"] }

# Ed25519 signing for audit log entries
ed25519-dalek = { version = "2", features = ["rand_core"] }

# BLAKE3 for content checksumming
blake3 = "1"

# Reed-Solomon erasure coding (RS 10+4)
reed-solomon-erasure = { version = "6", features = ["simd-accel"] }

# Cryptographically secure random number generation
rand    = "0.8"
rand_core = { version = "0.6", features = ["getrandom"] }
getrandom = { version = "0.2", features = ["js"] }
# ↑ "js" feature required: on WASM targets, getrandom pulls entropy
#   from the browser's crypto.getRandomValues(); on native it uses /dev/urandom.

# WASM bindings — generates the JS/TS glue code
wasm-bindgen      = "0.2"
wasm-bindgen-futures = "0.4"
js-sys            = "0.3"

# Error handling
thiserror = "1"

# Serialization (for key material export/import as structured bytes)
serde      = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1"

# Zero-on-drop memory safety for key material
zeroize = { version = "1", features = ["derive"] }

# ── Dev dependencies ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
[dev-dependencies]
wasm-bindgen-test = "0.3"  # Run tests in a headless browser or Node.js
hex               = "0.4"  # Hex encoding for test vector comparison

# ── Profile tuning ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
[profile.release]
# Critical: WASM output must be as small as possible for browser load time.
opt-level     = "z"   # Optimize for size over speed
lto           = true  # Link-time optimization
codegen-units = 1     # Single codegen unit for maximum LTO effectiveness
panic         = "abort"  # No unwinding in WASM — cuts ~10% bundle size

[profile.dev]
# Keep dev builds fast; use release only for wasm-pack
opt-level = 0
debug     = true
```

#### `packages/crypto-core/src/lib.rs`

```rust
//! aether-crypto-core
//!
//! Zero-knowledge cryptography pipeline for Project Aether.
//! Compiled to both native (rlib) and WASM (cdylib) targets.
//!
//! Architecture:
//!   password → [Argon2id] → MSK → [HKDF] → KEK → [AES-256-GCM] → encrypted_DEK
//!   DEK      → [AES-256-GCM(shard_bytes, AAD)] → ciphertext shard → provider
//!   DEK      → [Reed-Solomon 10+4] → 14 shards (10 data + 4 parity)

use wasm_bindgen::prelude::*;

pub mod encryption;
pub mod erasure;
pub mod kdf;
pub mod sharing;
pub mod audit;
pub mod error;

pub use error::CryptoError;

// ── WASM entry point ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

/// Called by the JS/TS layer once the WASM module has been instantiated.
/// Performs sanity checks on the WASM environment: verifies that `getrandom`
/// can pull entropy (will panic early in dev if the browser API is unavailable),
/// and returns the library version string for client-side version assertion.
///
/// Example (TypeScript):
/// ```ts
/// import init, { aether_init } from '@aether/crypto-core';
/// await init();
/// const version = aether_init();
/// console.assert(version.startsWith('0.1'), 'unexpected crypto-core version');
/// ```
#[wasm_bindgen]
pub fn aether_init() -> String {
    // Trigger a 1-byte random draw to validate the entropy source early.
    // On WASM: uses crypto.getRandomValues(). On native: uses /dev/urandom.
    // If this panics, the WASM runtime has no access to a CSPRNG — abort.
    let mut probe = [0u8; 1];
    getrandom::getrandom(&mut probe)
        .expect("aether-crypto-core: CSPRNG unavailable — cannot operate safely");

    format!(
        "aether-crypto-core/{} ({})",
        env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION"),
        if cfg!(target_arch = "wasm32") { "wasm32" } else { "native" }
    )
}
```

#### `packages/crypto-core/src/error.rs`

```rust
use thiserror::Error;

#[derive(Debug, Error)]
pub enum CryptoError {
    #[error("encryption failed: {0}")]
    EncryptionFailure(String),

    #[error("decryption failed — ciphertext may be corrupt or key is wrong")]
    DecryptionFailure,

    #[error("key derivation failed: {0}")]
    KdfFailure(String),

    #[error("erasure coding error: {0}")]
    ErasureError(String),

    #[error("invalid shard count: expected {expected}, got {got}")]
    InvalidShardCount { expected: usize, got: usize },

    #[error("CSPRNG unavailable")]
    EntropyUnavailable,

    #[error("sharing: {0}")]
    SharingError(String),
}

// Allow propagating CryptoError through wasm-bindgen boundaries as JsValue.
impl From<CryptoError> for wasm_bindgen::JsValue {
    fn from(e: CryptoError) -> Self {
        wasm_bindgen::JsValue::from_str(&e.to_string())
    }
}
```

#### `packages/crypto-core/src/kdf.rs` (stub — proves Argon2id + HKDF compile)

```rust
//! Key derivation: password → MSK → KEK → per-file DEKs.

use argon2::{Argon2, Params, Version, Algorithm};
use hkdf::Hkdf;
use sha2::Sha256;
use zeroize::{Zeroize, ZeroizeOnDrop};
use crate::error::CryptoError;

/// Master Secret Key — derived from the user's password on device.
/// Never leaves memory; zeroized on drop.
#[derive(ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct MasterSecretKey([u8; 32]);

/// Key Encryption Key — derived from MSK; used to wrap/unwrap DEKs.
#[derive(ZeroizeOnDrop)]
pub struct KeyEncryptionKey([u8; 32]);

/// Derive MSK from password using Argon2id.
///
/// Parameters match OWASP recommended minimum for interactive logins
/// while remaining within a 200ms budget on mid-range mobile hardware:
///   t = 3 iterations, m = 65536 KiB (64 MiB), p = 4 lanes.
pub fn derive_msk(password: &[u8], salt: &[u8; 32]) -> Result<MasterSecretKey, CryptoError> {
    let params = Params::new(
        65_536, // m_cost: 64 MiB
        3,      // t_cost: 3 passes
        4,      // p_cost: 4 parallel lanes
        Some(32),
    )
    .map_err(|e| CryptoError::KdfFailure(e.to_string()))?;

    let argon2 = Argon2::new(Algorithm::Argon2id, Version::V0x13, params);

    let mut output = [0u8; 32];
    argon2
        .hash_password_into(password, salt, &mut output)
        .map_err(|e| CryptoError::KdfFailure(e.to_string()))?;

    Ok(MasterSecretKey(output))
}

/// Derive KEK from MSK using HKDF-SHA256.
pub fn derive_kek(msk: &MasterSecretKey) -> KeyEncryptionKey {
    let hk = Hkdf::<Sha256>::new(None, &msk.0);
    let mut okm = [0u8; 32];
    hk.expand(b"aether_kek_v1", &mut okm)
        .expect("HKDF expand: 32-byte output is valid for SHA-256");
    KeyEncryptionKey(okm)
}

/// Derive a deterministic sub-key for a specific purpose (e.g., audit signing key).
pub fn derive_subkey(msk: &MasterSecretKey, info: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32] {
    let hk = Hkdf::<Sha256>::new(None, &msk.0);
    let mut okm = [0u8; 32];
    hk.expand(info, &mut okm)
        .expect("HKDF expand: 32-byte output is valid for SHA-256");
    okm
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn kdf_is_deterministic() {
        let password = b"correct-horse-battery-staple";
        let salt = [0xABu8; 32];

        let msk1 = derive_msk(password, &salt).unwrap();
        let msk2 = derive_msk(password, &salt).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(msk1.0, msk2.0, "Argon2id must be deterministic for same inputs");

        let kek1 = derive_kek(&msk1);
        let kek2 = derive_kek(&msk2);
        assert_eq!(kek1.0, kek2.0, "HKDF must be deterministic");
    }

    #[test]
    fn different_salts_produce_different_msks() {
        let password = b"same-password";
        let salt1 = [0x01u8; 32];
        let salt2 = [0x02u8; 32];
        let msk1 = derive_msk(password, &salt1).unwrap();
        let msk2 = derive_msk(password, &salt2).unwrap();
        assert_ne!(msk1.0, msk2.0);
    }
}
```

#### `packages/crypto-core/src/encryption.rs` (stub)

```rust
//! AES-256-GCM shard encryption/decryption.
//! Each shard is independently authenticated via AAD binding chunk and shard index.

use aes_gcm::{
    aead::{Aead, AeadCore, KeyInit, OsRng},
    Aes256Gcm, Key, Nonce,
};
use wasm_bindgen::prelude::*;
use crate::error::CryptoError;

/// AAD structure that binds a ciphertext to its exact position in the erasure matrix.
/// Prevents a compromised provider from swapping shard positions.
#[derive(Debug)]
pub struct ShardAad {
    pub inode_version_id: [u8; 16], // UUID bytes
    pub chunk_index:      u32,
    pub shard_index:      u32,
}

impl ShardAad {
    pub fn to_bytes(&self) -> [u8; 24] {
        let mut aad = [0u8; 24];
        aad[0..16].copy_from_slice(&self.inode_version_id);
        aad[16..20].copy_from_slice(&self.chunk_index.to_be_bytes());
        aad[20..24].copy_from_slice(&self.shard_index.to_be_bytes());
        aad
    }
}

/// Encrypt a shard with AES-256-GCM.
/// Returns `nonce || ciphertext_with_tag` as a single Vec for storage.
pub fn encrypt_shard(
    dek: &[u8; 32],
    plaintext: &[u8],
    aad: &ShardAad,
) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CryptoError> {
    let key    = Key::<Aes256Gcm>::from_slice(dek);
    let cipher = Aes256Gcm::new(key);
    let nonce  = Aes256Gcm::generate_nonce(&mut OsRng);

    let ciphertext = cipher
        .encrypt(&nonce, aes_gcm::aead::Payload {
            msg: plaintext,
            aad: &aad.to_bytes(),
        })
        .map_err(|_| CryptoError::EncryptionFailure("AES-GCM encrypt".into()))?;

    // Prepend 12-byte nonce: storage format is [nonce(12)] ++ [ciphertext+tag]
    let mut output = Vec::with_capacity(12 + ciphertext.len());
    output.extend_from_slice(&nonce);
    output.extend_from_slice(&ciphertext);
    Ok(output)
}

/// Decrypt a shard. Input must be `nonce || ciphertext_with_tag`.
pub fn decrypt_shard(
    dek: &[u8; 32],
    nonce_and_ciphertext: &[u8],
    aad: &ShardAad,
) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CryptoError> {
    if nonce_and_ciphertext.len() < 12 {
        return Err(CryptoError::DecryptionFailure);
    }
    let (nonce_bytes, ciphertext) = nonce_and_ciphertext.split_at(12);
    let key    = Key::<Aes256Gcm>::from_slice(dek);
    let cipher = Aes256Gcm::new(key);
    let nonce  = Nonce::from_slice(nonce_bytes);

    cipher
        .decrypt(nonce, aes_gcm::aead::Payload {
            msg: ciphertext,
            aad: &aad.to_bytes(),
        })
        .map_err(|_| CryptoError::DecryptionFailure)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn encrypt_decrypt_roundtrip() {
        let dek  = [0x42u8; 32];
        let data = b"shard payload data for chunk 0 shard 5";
        let aad  = ShardAad {
            inode_version_id: [0u8; 16],
            chunk_index: 0,
            shard_index: 5,
        };

        let encrypted = encrypt_shard(&dek, data, &aad).unwrap();
        let decrypted = decrypt_shard(&dek, &encrypted, &aad).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(decrypted, data);
    }

    #[test]
    fn wrong_aad_fails_authentication() {
        let dek  = [0x42u8; 32];
        let data = b"shard data";
        let aad  = ShardAad { inode_version_id: [0u8; 16], chunk_index: 0, shard_index: 0 };
        let bad_aad = ShardAad { inode_version_id: [0u8; 16], chunk_index: 0, shard_index: 1 };

        let encrypted = encrypt_shard(&dek, data, &aad).unwrap();
        // Swapping shard_index in AAD must fail decryption
        assert!(decrypt_shard(&dek, &encrypted, &bad_aad).is_err());
    }
}
```

#### `packages/crypto-core/src/erasure.rs` (stub)

```rust
//! Reed-Solomon RS(10, 4) erasure coding.
//! 10 data shards + 4 parity shards per logical chunk.

use reed_solomon_erasure::galois_8::ReedSolomon;
use crate::error::CryptoError;

pub const DATA_SHARDS:   usize = 10;
pub const PARITY_SHARDS: usize = 4;
pub const TOTAL_SHARDS:  usize = DATA_SHARDS + PARITY_SHARDS; // 14

/// Split `data` into DATA_SHARDS equal shards and compute PARITY_SHARDS.
/// Input length is padded to the nearest multiple of DATA_SHARDS.
///
/// Returns 14 equal-length byte vectors: [data_0..data_9, parity_0..parity_3].
pub fn encode(data: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<Vec<u8>>, CryptoError> {
    let r = ReedSolomon::new(DATA_SHARDS, PARITY_SHARDS)
        .map_err(|e| CryptoError::ErasureError(format!("RS init: {e:?}")))?;

    // Pad to multiple of DATA_SHARDS
    let shard_size = (data.len() + DATA_SHARDS - 1) / DATA_SHARDS;
    let mut shards: Vec<Vec<u8>> = (0..TOTAL_SHARDS)
        .map(|i| {
            let start = i * shard_size;
            let end   = ((i + 1) * shard_size).min(data.len());
            if i < DATA_SHARDS && start < data.len() {
                let mut shard = data[start..end].to_vec();
                shard.resize(shard_size, 0); // pad last shard
                shard
            } else {
                vec![0u8; shard_size]
            }
        })
        .collect();

    r.encode(&mut shards)
        .map_err(|e| CryptoError::ErasureError(format!("RS encode: {e:?}")))?;

    Ok(shards)
}

/// Reconstruct original data from a subset of shards.
/// `shards`: Vec of Option<Vec<u8>>; None = missing shard.
/// At least DATA_SHARDS (10) must be Some(_).
pub fn reconstruct(
    mut shards: Vec<Option<Vec<u8>>>,
) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CryptoError> {
    if shards.len() != TOTAL_SHARDS {
        return Err(CryptoError::InvalidShardCount {
            expected: TOTAL_SHARDS,
            got: shards.len(),
        });
    }

    let r = ReedSolomon::new(DATA_SHARDS, PARITY_SHARDS)
        .map_err(|e| CryptoError::ErasureError(format!("RS init: {e:?}")))?;

    r.reconstruct(&mut shards)
        .map_err(|e| CryptoError::ErasureError(format!("RS reconstruct: {e:?}")))?;

    // Flatten data shards back into contiguous bytes
    let data: Vec<u8> = shards[..DATA_SHARDS]
        .iter()
        .filter_map(|s| s.as_ref())
        .flat_map(|s| s.iter().copied())
        .collect();

    Ok(data)
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn encode_reconstruct_all_shards_present() {
        let original = b"The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog. Aether RS test.";
        let shards   = encode(original).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(shards.len(), TOTAL_SHARDS);

        let shard_opts: Vec<Option<Vec<u8>>> = shards.into_iter().map(Some).collect();
        let recovered = reconstruct(shard_opts).unwrap();
        assert!(recovered.starts_with(original));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reconstruct_with_four_missing_shards() {
        let original = b"RS(10,4): tolerate any 4 lost shards including full provider outage.";
        let shards   = encode(original).unwrap();

        // Simulate losing shards 2, 5, 11, 13 (mix of data and parity)
        let shard_opts: Vec<Option<Vec<u8>>> = shards
            .into_iter()
            .enumerate()
            .map(|(i, s)| if [2, 5, 11, 13].contains(&i) { None } else { Some(s) })
            .collect();

        let recovered = reconstruct(shard_opts).unwrap();
        assert!(recovered.starts_with(original));
    }

    #[test]
    fn reconstruct_fails_with_five_missing_shards() {
        let original = b"This should fail.";
        let shards   = encode(original).unwrap();

        // Lose 5 shards — exceeds RS(10,4) recovery capacity
        let shard_opts: Vec<Option<Vec<u8>>> = shards
            .into_iter()
            .enumerate()
            .map(|(i, s)| if i < 5 { None } else { Some(s) })
            .collect();

        assert!(reconstruct(shard_opts).is_err());
    }
}
```

## Project Aether — Phase 2: Auth Service, Storage Adapters & Google Drive

### Overview

Phase 2 delivers three things:

1. **`services/auth-service`** — Express/TypeScript service handling OAuth 2.0 PKCE for all 4 providers, JWT session issuance, and token refresh

2. **`packages/storage-adapters`** — Provider-agnostic `IStorageProvider` contract + shared types

3. **`packages/storage-adapters/src/providers/GoogleDriveAdapter.ts`** — First production adapter including upload, download, delete, delta-token tracking, and quota

#### `services/auth-service/src/config/providers.ts`

```typescript
// Provider OAuth 2.0 configuration.
// All secrets sourced from environment — never hardcoded.

import { z } from 'zod';

const ProviderConfigSchema = z.object({
  clientId:            z.string().min(1),
  clientSecret:        z.string().min(1),
  authorizationUrl:    z.string().url(),
  tokenUrl:            z.string().url(),
  revocationUrl:       z.string().url().optional(),
  scopes:              z.array(z.string()),
  redirectUri:         z.string().url(),
});

export type ProviderConfig = z.infer<typeof ProviderConfigSchema>;

function loadProvider(prefix: string, overrides: Partial<ProviderConfig>): ProviderConfig {
  return ProviderConfigSchema.parse({
    clientId:     process.env[`${prefix}_CLIENT_ID`],
    clientSecret: process.env[`${prefix}_CLIENT_SECRET`],
    redirectUri:  process.env[`${prefix}_REDIRECT_URI`] ??
                  `${process.env['APP_URL']}/auth/callback/${prefix.toLowerCase().replace('_', '-')}`,
    ...overrides,
  });
}

export const PROVIDER_CONFIGS = {
  google_drive: loadProvider('GOOGLE_DRIVE', {
    authorizationUrl: 'https://accounts.google.com/o/oauth2/v2/auth',
    tokenUrl:         'https://oauth2.googleapis.com/token',
    revocationUrl:    'https://oauth2.googleapis.com/revoke',
    scopes: [
      'https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive.file',
      'https://www.googleapis.com/auth/drive.metadata.readonly',
      'openid',
      'email',
    ],
  }),

  onedrive: loadProvider('ONEDRIVE', {
    authorizationUrl: 'https://login.microsoftonline.com/common/oauth2/v2.0/authorize',
    tokenUrl:         'https://login.microsoftonline.com/common/oauth2/v2.0/token',
    revocationUrl:    undefined, // Microsoft does not support token revocation endpoint
    scopes: [
      'Files.ReadWrite',
      'offline_access',
      'User.Read',
    ],
  }),

  dropbox: loadProvider('DROPBOX', {
    authorizationUrl: 'https://www.dropbox.com/oauth2/authorize',
    tokenUrl:         'https://api.dropboxapi.com/oauth2/token',
    revocationUrl:    'https://api.dropboxapi.com/2/auth/token/revoke',
    scopes: [
      'files.content.write',
      'files.content.read',
      'files.metadata.read',
      'account_info.read',
    ],
  }),

  mega: loadProvider('MEGA', {
    // MEGA does not use standard OAuth 2.0. It uses its own API key + email/password.
    // We model it as a pseudo-provider here for interface uniformity.
    // The "authorization URL" is our own UI route that collects MEGA credentials.
    authorizationUrl: `${process.env['APP_URL']}/auth/connect/mega`,
    tokenUrl:         `${process.env['APP_URL']}/auth/internal/mega-token`,
    scopes:           ['full_access'],
  }),
} as const;

export type ProviderId = keyof typeof PROVIDER_CONFIGS;
export const PROVIDER_IDS = Object.keys(PROVIDER_CONFIGS) as ProviderId[];
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/config/env.ts`

```typescript
import { z } from 'zod';

const EnvSchema = z.object({
  NODE_ENV:      z.enum(['development', 'test', 'production']).default('development'),
  PORT:          z.coerce.number().default(3001),
  APP_URL:       z.string().url(),
  DATABASE_URL:  z.string().min(1),
  REDIS_URL:     z.string().min(1),

  // JWT signing — RS256 keypair. In prod, private key lives in AWS Secrets Manager.
  JWT_PRIVATE_KEY_BASE64: z.string().min(1),
  JWT_PUBLIC_KEY_BASE64:  z.string().min(1),
  JWT_ISSUER:             z.string().default('https://api.aether.app'),
  JWT_ACCESS_TTL_SECONDS: z.coerce.number().default(900),    // 15 min
  JWT_REFRESH_TTL_DAYS:   z.coerce.number().default(30),

  // Server-side KEK for encrypting provider OAuth tokens at rest.
  // 32 bytes, base64-encoded. Rotate via key versioning in Secrets Manager.
  SERVER_TOKEN_KEK_BASE64: z.string().min(1),
  SERVER_TOKEN_KEK_VERSION: z.coerce.number().default(1),

  // PKCE state cookie signing secret (HMAC-SHA256)
  PKCE_STATE_SECRET: z.string().min(32),
});

function loadEnv() {
  const result = EnvSchema.safeParse(process.env);
  if (!result.success) {
    console.error('❌ Invalid environment configuration:');
    console.error(result.error.flatten().fieldErrors);
    process.exit(1);
  }
  return result.data;
}

export const env = loadEnv();
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/types/index.ts`

```typescript
export type ProviderId = 'google_drive' | 'onedrive' | 'dropbox' | 'mega';

export interface OAuthTokenSet {
  accessToken:           string;
  refreshToken:          string | null;
  expiresAt:             Date;
  scopes:                string[];
}

// Shape stored in Redis during the PKCE challenge window (TTL: 10 minutes)
export interface PkceState {
  codeVerifier:  string;       // S256 challenge plaintext; 43–128 chars
  providerId:    ProviderId;
  userId:        string;       // UUID of the authenticated Aether user
  redirectAfter: string;       // client-side URL to redirect to on completion
  createdAt:     number;       // epoch ms
}

export interface AetherSession {
  userId:    string;
  email:     string;
  iat:       number;
  exp:       number;
}

export interface ConnectedProvider {
  providerId:           ProviderId;
  connectedAt:          string;
  storageQuotaBytes:    number | null;
  storageUsedBytes:     number | null;
  lastRefreshedAt:      string | null;
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/services/pkce.ts`

```typescript
// PKCE (RFC 7636) helper — S256 method only.
// Code verifier → SHA-256 → base64url → code challenge sent to provider.
// Verifier is stored server-side in Redis; never sent to the provider.

import crypto from 'node:crypto';

/** Generate a cryptographically random code verifier (43–128 URL-safe chars). */
export function generateCodeVerifier(): string {
  return crypto.randomBytes(64).toString('base64url');
}

/** Derive the S256 code challenge from a verifier. */
export function deriveCodeChallenge(verifier: string): string {
  return crypto
    .createHash('sha256')
    .update(verifier)
    .digest('base64url');
}

/** Generate a state token for CSRF protection. */
export function generateStateToken(): string {
  return crypto.randomBytes(32).toString('base64url');
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/services/token-encryption.ts`

```typescript
// AES-256-GCM encryption for OAuth tokens stored in provider_credentials.
// Uses the SERVER_TOKEN_KEK (not the user's ZK key — this is server-side).
// Tokens must be decryptable by background services for delta-sync and repair.

import crypto from 'node:crypto';
import { env } from '../config/env.js';

const ALGORITHM  = 'aes-256-gcm' as const;
const NONCE_SIZE = 12; // 96-bit recommended for GCM
const TAG_SIZE   = 16;

export interface EncryptedBlob {
  ciphertext: Buffer;  // nonce(12) + ciphertext + authTag(16)
  keyVersion: number;
}

export function encryptTokens(plaintext: string): EncryptedBlob {
  const kek    = Buffer.from(env.SERVER_TOKEN_KEK_BASE64, 'base64');
  const nonce  = crypto.randomBytes(NONCE_SIZE);
  const cipher = crypto.createCipheriv(ALGORITHM, kek, nonce);

  const encrypted = Buffer.concat([
    cipher.update(plaintext, 'utf8'),
    cipher.final(),
  ]);
  const tag = cipher.getAuthTag();

  return {
    ciphertext: Buffer.concat([nonce, encrypted, tag]),
    keyVersion: env.SERVER_TOKEN_KEK_VERSION,
  };
}

export function decryptTokens(blob: EncryptedBlob): string {
  const kek   = Buffer.from(env.SERVER_TOKEN_KEK_BASE64, 'base64');
  const buf   = blob.ciphertext;

  if (buf.length < NONCE_SIZE + TAG_SIZE) {
    throw new Error('EncryptedBlob too short — corrupt data');
  }

  const nonce      = buf.subarray(0, NONCE_SIZE);
  const tag        = buf.subarray(buf.length - TAG_SIZE);
  const ciphertext = buf.subarray(NONCE_SIZE, buf.length - TAG_SIZE);

  const decipher = crypto.createDecipheriv(ALGORITHM, kek, nonce);
  decipher.setAuthTag(tag);

  return (
    decipher.update(ciphertext, undefined, 'utf8') +
    decipher.final('utf8')
  );
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/services/jwt.ts`

```typescript
// RS256 JWT issuance and verification using the `jose` library.
// Access tokens: short-lived (15 min), signed with private key.
// Refresh tokens: long-lived (30 days), stored as opaque references in Redis.

import { SignJWT, jwtVerify, importPKCS8, importSPKI } from 'jose';
import type { KeyLike } from 'jose';
import crypto from 'node:crypto';
import { env } from '../config/env.js';
import type { AetherSession } from '../types/index.js';

let _privateKey: KeyLike | null = null;
let _publicKey:  KeyLike | null = null;

async function getPrivateKey(): Promise<KeyLike> {
  if (!_privateKey) {
    const pem = Buffer.from(env.JWT_PRIVATE_KEY_BASE64, 'base64').toString('utf8');
    _privateKey = await importPKCS8(pem, 'RS256');
  }
  return _privateKey;
}

async function getPublicKey(): Promise<KeyLike> {
  if (!_publicKey) {
    const pem = Buffer.from(env.JWT_PUBLIC_KEY_BASE64, 'base64').toString('utf8');
    _publicKey = await importSPKI(pem, 'RS256');
  }
  return _publicKey;
}

export async function signAccessToken(
  payload: Omit<AetherSession, 'iat' | 'exp'>,
): Promise<string> {
  const key = await getPrivateKey();
  return new SignJWT({ ...payload })
    .setProtectedHeader({ alg: 'RS256' })
    .setIssuedAt()
    .setIssuer(env.JWT_ISSUER)
    .setAudience('aether:api')
    .setExpirationTime(`${env.JWT_ACCESS_TTL_SECONDS}s`)
    .sign(key);
}

export async function verifyAccessToken(token: string): Promise<AetherSession> {
  const key = await getPublicKey();
  const { payload } = await jwtVerify(token, key, {
    issuer:   env.JWT_ISSUER,
    audience: 'aether:api',
  });
  return payload as unknown as AetherSession;
}

/** Opaque refresh token — a random reference stored in Redis pointing to userId. */
export function generateRefreshToken(): string {
  return crypto.randomBytes(40).toString('base64url');
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/services/session-store.ts`

```typescript
// Redis-backed session store for:
//   - PKCE state (TTL: 10 min, keyed by state token)
//   - Refresh tokens (TTL: 30 days, keyed by token hash)

import Redis from 'ioredis';
import crypto from 'node:crypto';
import { env } from '../config/env.js';
import type { PkceState } from '../types/index.js';

export const redis = new Redis(env.REDIS_URL, {
  maxRetriesPerRequest: 3,
  enableReadyCheck:     true,
  lazyConnect:          false,
});

redis.on('error', (err) => {
  console.error('[redis] connection error:', err);
});

const PKCE_TTL_SECONDS    = 10 * 60;          // 10 minutes
const REFRESH_TTL_SECONDS = 30 * 24 * 60 * 60; // 30 days

// ── PKCE state ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export async function savePkceState(
  stateToken: string,
  state: PkceState,
): Promise<void> {
  await redis.setex(
    `pkce:${stateToken}`,
    PKCE_TTL_SECONDS,
    JSON.stringify(state),
  );
}

export async function consumePkceState(
  stateToken: string,
): Promise<PkceState | null> {
  const key  = `pkce:${stateToken}`;
  const raw  = await redis.getdel(key); // atomic get + delete; one-time use
  if (!raw) return null;
  return JSON.parse(raw) as PkceState;
}

// ── Refresh tokens ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

export async function saveRefreshToken(
  token: string,
  userId: string,
): Promise<void> {
  const hashed = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(token).digest('hex');
  await redis.setex(
    `refresh:${hashed}`,
    REFRESH_TTL_SECONDS,
    userId,
  );
}

export async function resolveRefreshToken(
  token: string,
): Promise<string | null> {
  const hashed = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(token).digest('hex');
  return redis.get(`refresh:${hashed}`);
}

export async function revokeRefreshToken(token: string): Promise<void> {
  const hashed = crypto.createHash('sha256').update(token).digest('hex');
  await redis.del(`refresh:${hashed}`);
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/services/credential-store.ts`

```typescript
// Persists encrypted OAuth tokens to CockroachDB provider_credentials table.

import pg from 'pg';
import { env } from '../config/env.js';
import { encryptTokens, decryptTokens } from './token-encryption.js';
import type { ProviderId, OAuthTokenSet, ConnectedProvider } from '../types/index.js';

const { Pool } = pg;

export const db = new Pool({
  connectionString: env.DATABASE_URL,
  max:              20,
  idleTimeoutMillis: 30_000,
});

export async function upsertProviderCredentials(
  userId: string,
  providerId: ProviderId,
  tokens: OAuthTokenSet,
): Promise<void> {
  const plaintext = JSON.stringify(tokens);
  const blob      = encryptTokens(plaintext);

  await db.query(
    `INSERT INTO provider_credentials
       (user_id, provider_id, encrypted_tokens, token_nonce, scopes, last_refreshed_at)
     VALUES ($1, $2, $3, $4, $5, now())
     ON CONFLICT (user_id, provider_id) DO UPDATE SET
       encrypted_tokens  = EXCLUDED.encrypted_tokens,
       token_nonce       = EXCLUDED.token_nonce,
       scopes            = EXCLUDED.scopes,
       last_refreshed_at = now(),
       is_active         = true`,
    [
      userId,
      providerId,
      blob.ciphertext,
      // token_nonce is embedded inside ciphertext for our scheme;
      // pass a zero-byte placeholder to satisfy the NOT NULL column constraint.
      Buffer.alloc(12),
      tokens.scopes,
    ],
  );
}

export async function getProviderTokens(
  userId: string,
  providerId: ProviderId,
): Promise<OAuthTokenSet | null> {
  const { rows } = await db.query<{
    encrypted_tokens: Buffer;
    key_version:      number;
  }>(
    `SELECT encrypted_tokens FROM provider_credentials
     WHERE user_id = $1 AND provider_id = $2 AND is_active = true`,
    [userId, providerId],
  );

  if (rows.length === 0) return null;

  const row  = rows[0]!;
  const json = decryptTokens({
    ciphertext: row.encrypted_tokens,
    keyVersion: env.SERVER_TOKEN_KEK_VERSION,
  });

  return JSON.parse(json) as OAuthTokenSet;
}

export async function listConnectedProviders(
  userId: string,
): Promise<ConnectedProvider[]> {
  const { rows } = await db.query<{
    provider_id:          string;
    connected_at:         string;
    storage_quota_bytes:  string | null;
    storage_used_bytes:   string | null;
    last_refreshed_at:    string | null;
  }>(
    `SELECT provider_id, connected_at, storage_quota_bytes,
            storage_used_bytes, last_refreshed_at
     FROM provider_credentials
     WHERE user_id = $1 AND is_active = true
     ORDER BY connected_at ASC`,
    [userId],
  );

  return rows.map((r) => ({
    providerId:           r.provider_id as ProviderId,
    connectedAt:          r.connected_at,
    storageQuotaBytes:    r.storage_quota_bytes ? Number(r.storage_quota_bytes) : null,
    storageUsedBytes:     r.storage_used_bytes  ? Number(r.storage_used_bytes)  : null,
    lastRefreshedAt:      r.last_refreshed_at,
  }));
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/middleware/authenticate.ts`

```typescript
// JWT bearer token verification middleware.
// Attaches verified session payload to req.session.

import type { Request, Response, NextFunction } from 'express';
import { verifyAccessToken } from '../services/jwt.js';
import type { AetherSession } from '../types/index.js';

declare global {
  namespace Express {
    interface Request {
      session?: AetherSession;
    }
  }
}

export async function authenticate(
  req: Request,
  res: Response,
  next: NextFunction,
): Promise<void> {
  const header = req.headers['authorization'];

  if (!header?.startsWith('Bearer ')) {
    res.status(401).json({ error: 'Missing or malformed Authorization header' });
    return;
  }

  const token = header.slice(7);

  try {
    req.session = await verifyAccessToken(token);
    next();
  } catch {
    res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid or expired access token' });
  }
}
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/routes/oauth.ts`

```typescript
// OAuth 2.0 PKCE routes.
//
// Flow:
//   GET  /auth/connect/:providerId          → redirect to provider's auth URL
//   GET  /auth/callback/:providerId         → exchange code, store tokens, issue JWT
//   POST /auth/refresh                      → rotate refresh token, issue new access token
//   POST /auth/disconnect/:providerId       → revoke tokens, mark credential inactive
//   GET  /auth/providers                    → list connected providers for authed user

import { Router, type Request, type Response } from 'express';
import axios from 'axios';
import {
  generateCodeVerifier,
  deriveCodeChallenge,
  generateStateToken,
} from '../services/pkce.js';
import {
  savePkceState,
  consumePkceState,
  saveRefreshToken,
  resolveRefreshToken,
  revokeRefreshToken,
} from '../services/session-store.js';
import {
  upsertProviderCredentials,
  getProviderTokens,
  listConnectedProviders,
} from '../services/credential-store.js';
import {
  signAccessToken,
  generateRefreshToken,
} from '../services/jwt.js';
import { PROVIDER_CONFIGS, PROVIDER_IDS } from '../config/providers.js';
import { authenticate } from '../middleware/authenticate.js';
import type { ProviderId, OAuthTokenSet } from '../types/index.js';

export const oauthRouter = Router();

// ── Connect: initiate PKCE flow ───────────────────────────────────────────────

oauthRouter.get(
  '/connect/:providerId',
  authenticate,
  async (req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> => {
    const providerId = req.params['providerId'] as ProviderId;

    if (!PROVIDER_IDS.includes(providerId)) {
      res.status(400).json({ error: `Unknown provider: ${providerId}` });
      return;
    }

    const config        = PROVIDER_CONFIGS[providerId];
    const codeVerifier  = generateCodeVerifier();
    const codeChallenge = deriveCodeChallenge(codeVerifier);
    const stateToken    = generateStateToken();

    await savePkceState(stateToken, {
      codeVerifier,
      providerId,
      userId:        req.session!.userId,
      redirectAfter: (req.query['redirectAfter'] as string) ?? '/',
      createdAt:     Date.now(),
    });

    const params = new URLSearchParams({
      client_id:             config.clientId,
      redirect_uri:          config.redirectUri,
      response_type:         'code',
      scope:                 config.scopes.join(' '),
      state:                 stateToken,
      code_challenge:        codeChallenge,
      code_challenge_method: 'S256',
      access_type:           'offline', // Google: forces refresh_token issuance
      prompt:                'consent', // Google: re-consent to get refresh_token on reconnect
    });

    res.redirect(`${config.authorizationUrl}?${params.toString()}`);
  },
);

// ── Callback: exchange code for tokens ───────────────────────────────────────

oauthRouter.get(
  '/callback/:providerId',
  async (req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> => {
    const providerId = req.params['providerId'] as ProviderId;
    const { code, state, error } = req.query as Record<string, string>;

    if (error) {
      res.redirect(`/?error=${encodeURIComponent(error)}`);
      return;
    }

    if (!code || !state) {
      res.status(400).json({ error: 'Missing code or state parameter' });
      return;
    }

    const pkceState = await consumePkceState(state);
    if (!pkceState || pkceState.providerId !== providerId) {
      res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid or expired state token' });
      return;
    }

    const config = PROVIDER_CONFIGS[providerId];

    let tokenResponse: {
      access_token:  string;
      refresh_token?: string;
      expires_in:    number;
      scope?:        string;
    };

    try {
      const resp = await axios.post(
        config.tokenUrl,
        new URLSearchParams({
          client_id:     config.clientId,
          client_secret: config.clientSecret,
          code,
          redirect_uri:  config.redirectUri,
          grant_type:    'authorization_code',
          code_verifier: pkceState.codeVerifier,
        }),
        { headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/x-www-form-urlencoded' } },
      );
      tokenResponse = resp.data as typeof tokenResponse;
    } catch (err) {
      console.error(`[oauth] token exchange failed for ${providerId}:`, err);
      res.status(502).json({ error: 'Token exchange with provider failed' });
      return;
    }

    const tokens: OAuthTokenSet = {
      accessToken:  tokenResponse.access_token,
      refreshToken: tokenResponse.refresh_token ?? null,
      expiresAt:    new Date(Date.now() + tokenResponse.expires_in * 1000),
      scopes:       tokenResponse.scope?.split(' ') ?? config.scopes,
    };

    await upsertProviderCredentials(pkceState.userId, providerId, tokens);

    res.redirect(pkceState.redirectAfter);
  },
);

// ── Refresh access token ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

oauthRouter.post(
  '/refresh',
  async (req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> => {
    const { refreshToken } = req.body as { refreshToken?: string };

    if (!refreshToken) {
      res.status(400).json({ error: 'refreshToken required' });
      return;
    }

    const userId = await resolveRefreshToken(refreshToken);
    if (!userId) {
      res.status(401).json({ error: 'Invalid or expired refresh token' });
      return;
    }

    // Rotate: revoke old, issue new pair
    await revokeRefreshToken(refreshToken);
    const newRefreshToken = generateRefreshToken();
    await saveRefreshToken(newRefreshToken, userId);

    const accessToken = await signAccessToken({ userId, email: '' });

    res.json({ accessToken, refreshToken: newRefreshToken });
  },
);

// ── Disconnect provider ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

oauthRouter.post(
  '/disconnect/:providerId',
  authenticate,
  async (req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> => {
    const providerId = req.params['providerId'] as ProviderId;
    const userId     = req.session!.userId;

    const tokens = await getProviderTokens(userId, providerId);
    if (!tokens) {
      res.status(404).json({ error: 'Provider not connected' });
      return;
    }

    const config = PROVIDER_CONFIGS[providerId];

    // Best-effort revocation — provider may have already expired the token
    if (config.revocationUrl && tokens.refreshToken) {
      try {
        await axios.post(config.revocationUrl, null, {
          params: { token: tokens.refreshToken },
        });
      } catch {
        // Non-fatal; we still mark the credential inactive locally
      }
    }

    // Soft-revoke in DB
    const { db } = await import('../services/credential-store.js');
    await db.query(
      `UPDATE provider_credentials SET is_active = false
       WHERE user_id = $1 AND provider_id = $2`,
      [userId, providerId],
    );

    res.json({ ok: true });
  },
);

// ── List connected providers ──────────────────────────────────────────────────

oauthRouter.get(
  '/providers',
  authenticate,
  async (req: Request, res: Response): Promise<void> => {
    const providers = await listConnectedProviders(req.session!.userId);
    res.json({ providers });
  },
);
```

#### `services/auth-service/src/index.ts`

```typescript
import express from 'express';
import helmet from 'helmet';
import cors from 'cors';
import cookieParser from 'cookie-parser';
import rateLimit from 'express-rate-limit';
import { oauthRouter } from './routes/oauth.js';
import { env } from './config/env.js';
import { redis } from './services/session-store.js';
import { db } from './services/credential-store.js';

const app = express();

// ── Security middleware ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

app.use(helmet());
app.use(cors({
  origin:      env.APP_URL,
  credentials: true,
}));
app.use(express.json({ limit: '16kb' }));
app.use(cookieParser());

// Strict rate limiting on auth endpoints
app.use('/auth', rateLimit({
  windowMs: 15 * 60 * 1000, // 15 minutes
  max:      50,
  message:  { error: 'Too many requests from this IP, retry after 15 minutes' },
}));

// ── Routes ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

app.use('/auth', oauthRouter);

app.get('/health', (_req, res) => {
  res.json({ status: 'ok', service: 'auth-service', ts: new Date().toISOString() });
});

// ── Startup ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

async function start() {
  // Verify DB connectivity before accepting traffic
  await db.query('SELECT 1');
  console.log('[auth-service] CockroachDB connected');

  await redis.ping();
  console.log('[auth-service] Redis/Dragonfly connected');

  app.listen(env.PORT, () => {
    console.log(`[auth-service] Listening on :${env.PORT} (${env.NODE_ENV})`);
  });
}

process.on('SIGTERM', async () => {
  console.log('[auth-service] SIGTERM received, draining...');
  await db.end();
  await redis.quit();
  process.exit(0);
});

start().catch((err) => {
  console.error('[auth-service] Fatal startup error:', err);
  process.exit(1);
});
```
